Actually the input rows are [[1, 2], [3, 4]]. If we mark the last row as total, maybe it should show the sum of each column? But the expected second row is \"TOTAL,3,4\". 3 and 4 are not sums; they are the values of the last row? Wait, the last row is [3,4]. So marking it as total might just prefix \"TOTAL\" to the row, keeping the original values. Or maybe it should render the total row computed by column_totals? The test expects \"TOTAL,3,4\". That is \"TOTAL\" then the values of the last row? Or the totals of the columns? Let's see: column_totals of [[1,2],[3,4]] would be [4,6]. But the test expects 3,4. So it's not using column_totals. It's just taking the last row's values.\n\nThus the test seems to want: if mark_last=True, then the last row is rendered as \"TOTAL\" concatenated with the row values.\n\nSo we need to modify render_rows to accept mark_last=False by default, and if True, for the last row, prepend \"TOTAL\".\n\n3. export_csv should accept totals=True to append a total row. The test expects \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". That is the original rows rendered, plus a total row computed by column_totals([1,2],[3,4]) = [4,6], and rendered as \"TOTAL,4,6\". So export_csv should if totals=True, append a row that is [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows). But note the expected total row is \"TOTAL,4,6\", not \"TOTAL,4,6\" with the totals appended after the original columns. The original rows have 2 columns. The total row has 3 columns: \"TOTAL\" plus the totals of each column. So the total row has one more column than the data rows? Actually the data rows are [1,2] and [3,4] — two columns. The total row is [4,6] — two numbers. But the expected string is \"TOTAL,4,6\" — that's three fields: TOTAL, 4, 6. So it's \"TOTAL\" followed by the two totals. So the total row has the same number of columns as the data rows, but with an extra \"TOTAL\" label at the beginning. Or maybe the data rows should have a \"TOTAL\" column added? But the original rows have two columns. So the CSV would have varying number of columns? That seems odd.
